            if r:
                L += tex_sitedev(fs, pre, pre, [[x['violin']] + x['projs'] for x in r[0]], 'Site deviation from pure LLZO')
                L += tex_sitedev(fs, pre, pre, [x['full'] for x in r[0]], 'Whole-cell projection')
        L += tex_tables((tabs or {}).get(gi, []))
    if pool:
        L += ['\\clearpage', '\\section*{Shell density vs MSD and $H_R$ (pooled over folders)}', '', tex_txt(pool['text']), '']
        for pth in pool['figs']: L += tex_blocks(fs, 'shellpool', [pth], False, 0.8)
    L.append('\\end{document}')
    tex = os.path.join(outdir, base + '.tex')
    with open(tex, 'w') as fh: fh.write('\n'.join(L) + '\n')
    return tex


def make_tables(groups, sd, p, cm):
    &amp;quot;&amp;quot;&amp;quot;{group index: blocks} comparison + deviation tables (shared by docx and tex).&amp;quot;&amp;quot;&amp;quot;
    tabs = {}
    for gi, g in enumerate(groups):
        ok, dev, B = [], [], []
        for f in g['folders']:
            if not os.path.isdir(f): continue
            ok.append(f)
            r = sd.get((gi, f))
            if r: dev.append((fname(f), r[1]))
        if len(ok) &amp;lt; 2:
            warn(f'group {gi + 1}: &amp;lt;2 valid folders, comparison skipped')
        else:
            systems = load_systems(ok, gi, cm)
            if len(systems) &amp;lt; 2: warn(f'group {gi + 1}: &amp;lt;2 systems with readable 05/07 logs, comparison skipped')
            else: B += compare_blocks(systems)
        if dev: B += dev_blocks(dev, p['edges'])
        tabs[gi] = B
    return tabs


def build_report(groups, sd, p, rm=None, tabs=None, rho=None, pool=None):
    rm, tabs, rho = rm or {}, tabs or {}, rho or {}
    doc = Document()
    sec = doc.sections[0]
    sec.orientation = WD_ORIENT.LANDSCAPE
    sec.page_width, sec.page_height = Cm(PAGE_W), Cm(PAGE_H)
    sec.left_margin = sec.right_margin = sec.top_margin = sec.bottom_margin = Cm(MARGIN)

    first = True
    for gi, g in enumerate(groups):
        for f in g['folders']:
            if not os.path.isdir(f):
                warn(f'not a folder, skipped: {f}'); continue
            r = sd.get((gi, f))
            add_folder(doc, f, page_break=not first, sd=r[0] if r else None, rmsd=rm.get((gi, f)), rho=rho.get((gi, f)))
            first = False
        render_docx(doc, tabs.get(gi, []))
    if pool:
        h = doc.add_heading('Shell density vs MSD and H_R (pooled over folders)', 1)
        h.paragraph_format.page_break_before = True
        doc.add_paragraph(pool['text'])
        for pth in pool['figs']: add_images(doc, [pth], False, MAX_H - 3.0)
    return doc


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- main
def main():
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__, formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter)
    ap.add_argument('items', nargs='+', help='groups: {A B C} {D E Ga=Li24d}')
    ap.add_argument('-o', '--out', default='report.docx')
    ap.add_argument('--scripts', help='folder with llzo_io.py, llzo_sites.py ... (default: ./with_flag next to this file)')
    ap.add_argument('--cif', default=CIF_DEFAULT, help='pristine Ia-3d garnet CIF (Li, La, Zr, O)')
    ap.add_argument('--T', type=int, nargs='*', help='temperatures for the site deviation (default: all dumps found)')
    ap.add_argument('--tmin', type=float, help='analysis window start, ps (default: tmin_ps of the settings)')
    ap.add_argument('--shells', type=float, nargs='*', default=list(lio.DEFAULT_SHELLS), help='shell edges, A (as 05)')
    ap.add_argument('--every', type=int, default=10, help='use every Nth frame of the window')
    ap.add_argument('--window', type=float, default=8.0, help='projection half-width in-plane, A')
    ap.add_argument('--slab', type=float, default=1.5, help='projection slab half-thickness, A')
    ap.add_argument('--jobs', type=int, default=1, help='parallel (folder, T) computations')
    ap.add_argument('--tau-ps', type=float, default=1.0, help='return window for committed hops, ps (frames = tau / dump frame spacing)')
    ap.add_argument('--rho-r', type=float, default=4.0, help='local Li density: neighbours within this radius, A')
    ap.add_argument('--rho-bins', type=int, default=5, help='local Li density: number of fixed-width bins of relative local density (0.1-1.5)')
    ap.add_argument('--rho-lags', type=float, nargs='*', default=[1.0, 5.0, 10.0], help='local Li density: tau values, ps')
    ap.add_argument('--pool-lags', type=float, nargs='*', default=[2.0, 5.0, 10.0], help='shell density pool: tau values, ps')
    ap.add_argument('--pool-min-n', type=float, default=3.0, help='shell density pool: shells with fewer Li (mean per origin) are pale / not correlated')
    ap.add_argument('--ctrl-n', type=int, default=10, help='shell density pool: number of non-dopant control reference points')
    ap.add_argument('--ctrl-ref', choices=['li', 'random'], default='li', help='control points: positions of random Li atoms at frame 0 (li) or random points')
    ap.add_argument('--ctrl-seed', type=int, default=0, help='shell density pool: random seed of the control points')
    ap.add_argument('--rad-lags', type=float, nargs='*', default=[2.0, 5.0, 10.0, 20.0, 30.0], help='radius of influence: tau values, ps (a tau needs a window of at least 2 x blocks x tau frames, else it is skipped with a warning)')
    ap.add_argument('--rad-bw', type=float, default=0.5, help='radius of influence: Gaussian kernel width, A')
    ap.add_argument('--rad-step', type=float, default=0.25, help='radius of influence: grid step, A')
    ap.add_argument('--rad-rmax', type=float, default=12.0, help='radius of influence: largest distance on the grid, A')
    ap.add_argument('--rad-far', type=float, help='radius of influence: far-Li normaliser = Li farther than this from every dopant (default: last shell edge)')
    ap.add_argument('--rad-boot', type=int, default=200, help='radius of influence: block-bootstrap replicates (element level)')
    ap.add_argument('--rad-min-neff', type=float, default=100.0, help='radius of influence: grid points with fewer kernel-weighted Li-origins are dropped')
    ap.add_argument('--rad-rmin', type=float, default=2.5, help='radius of influence: fit only r &amp;gt;= this, A (0 = whole profile)')
    ap.add_argument('--rad-tref', type=int, help='radius of influence: T of the summary figure and the printed table (default: highest T)')
    ap.add_argument('--rad-model', choices=['exp', 'gauss', 'sigmoid'], default='sigmoid', help='radius of influence: model of the r95 vs T panel')
    ap.add_argument('--radius-only', action='store_true', help='compute only the radius of influence (no docx / tex / pdf, nothing else recomputed)')
    ap.add_argument('--no-radius', action='store_true', help='skip the radius of influence')
    ap.add_argument('--recompute', action='store_true', help='ignore cached sitedev, rmsd and local-density results')
    lio.add_config_args(ap)
    a = ap.parse_args()
    groups = parse_groups(a.items)
    if not groups: sys.exit('no groups given')
    for g in groups: g['folders'] = expand(g['folders'])

    p = dict(cif=a.cif, every=a.every, tmin=a.tmin, edges=list(a.shells), minframes=3, boot=1000, window=a.window, slab=a.slab)
    outdir_rad = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(a.out)), 'radius')
    if a.radius_only:
        make_radius(run_radius(groups, a, p), outdir_rad, a, p)
        return
    sd = {}
    if any(g['sites'] for g in groups):
        if not os.path.exists(a.cif): warn(f'CIF not found, site deviation skipped: {a.cif}')
        else: sd = run_sitedev(groups, a, p)
    rm = run_rmsd(groups, a, p)
    cm = run_commit(groups, a, p)
    rh = run_rho(groups, a, p)
    sp = run_shellpool(groups, a, p)
    pool = make_pool(sp, os.path.join(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(a.out)), 'shellpool'), a.pool_min_n)
    if not a.no_radius: make_radius(run_radius(groups, a, p), outdir_rad, a, p)
    tabs = make_tables(groups, sd, p, cm)

    global SVG_MODE
    SVG_MODE = False
    doc = build_report(groups, sd, p, rm, tabs, rh, pool)
    doc.save(a.out)
    print(f'wrote {os.path.abspath(a.out)}  ({len(groups)} group(s), png)')
    tex = write_tex(groups, sd, rm, a.out, tabs, rh, pool)
    print(f'wrote {tex}  (figures in {os.path.join(os.path.dirname(tex), FIGDIR)}/)')
    # pdf: same report built again with svg pictures, converted by LibreOffice (vector)
    soffice = shutil.which('soffice') or shutil.which('libreoffice')
    if not soffice: sys.exit('soffice/libreoffice not found -&amp;gt; no pdf')
    import tempfile
    SVG_MODE = True
    base = os.path.splitext(os.path.basename(a.out))[0]
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:
        tdoc = os.path.join(tmp, base + '.docx')
        build_report(groups, sd, p, rm, tabs, rh, pool).save(tdoc)
        subprocess.run([soffice, '--headless', '--convert-to', 'pdf', '--outdir', tmp, tdoc], check=True)
        pdf = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(a.out)), base + '.pdf')
        shutil.move(os.path.join(tmp, base + '.pdf'), pdf)
    if SVG_MISSING: print(f'note: {len(SVG_MISSING)} image(s) have no .svg next to the .png -&amp;gt; PNG used in pdf, e.g. {SVG_MISSING[0]}', file=sys.stderr)
    print(f'wrote {pdf}  (svg)')


if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()

Still working on build_doc.py: the plain-MSD fit logic is in; now I'm updating the figures, the csv columns and the output folder (radius_plainmsd/), then I'll test it.

The edited `build_doc.py` now also fits the plain MSD(r) in Å², with no division by the far MSD, and writes it to `radius_plainmsd/` next to the existing `radius/`. I tested it on synthetic data only, not on your trajectories, since the dumps and the other scripts aren't here. On a known profile both fits recovered the same shape (A 0.69, r0 3.52, r95 5.28), with the plateau B at 1.0 for the ratio and 4.0 Å² for the plain fit.

**How it works**
- The plain fit reuses the kernel sums that `run_radius` already computes or caches, so there is no extra trajectory pass. `radius_both()` runs the ratio fit and then the plain fit; `--radius-only` does both too.
- The plateau B is free in Å², between 0.5 and 3 times the mean of the outermost 3 fitted points. A, r50, r90 and r95 are relative to the plateau, so they compare directly with the ratio fit.
- `radius_plainmsd/` has the same file names as `radius/`. In the profile csv the `ratio` columns become `msd_A2`, and a `far_msd_A2` column is added for reference.
- In the plain figures, the control null band is the mean ±2 sd of the control profiles. A grey line marks the far-Li MSD for reference only; it is not used in the fit.
- `--no-plain` skips the plain fit.

**Model-free numbers**
- r_noise, ell, V and r_eff need a dimensionless deficit, so the plain version divides the profile by the mean of its own outermost 5 valid points instead of the far-Li MSD.
- Treat those four as relative to the profile's outer level when you compare them with the ratio version.

- **Window length:** a lag only runs if the analysis window (from `--tmin` to the end of the dump) is longer than the lag plus 10 frames. A lag that doesn't fit is skipped with a `radius: window too short for tau = [...] ps` warning. If your runs are shorter than about 100 ps after `tmin`, the 100 ps column won't appear; lower `--tmin` or use a smaller maximum lag.
- **Recompute:** the cache key includes the lag list, so changing it makes every (folder, T) recompute. Use `--jobs N` to parallelise.
- **Memory:** every lag keeps a frames × Li-atoms array in memory, so the extra lags add some RAM per job.
- **More plots:** each lag gets its own row in the `radius_profiles_T&amp;lt;T&amp;gt;K.png` figures and its own `radius_summary_tau&amp;lt;tau&amp;gt;ps.png`, in both `radius/` and `radius_plainmsd/`. With 7 lags the profile figures get tall.
- **Large radii:** at long lags the suppression zone often reaches past `--rad-rmax` (12 Å). If r95 shows a `*` (extrapolated), also pass `--rad-rmax 16` or higher, provided the box is big enough (the radius can't be trusted beyond half the box).&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/section&gt;&lt;section class=&quot;msg user&quot;&gt;&lt;h3&gt;User&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;div class=&quot;text&quot;&gt;&lt;pre&gt;in only MSD fit, what does the plateu tell us&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/section&gt;&lt;section class=&quot;msg assistant&quot;&gt;&lt;h3&gt;Assistant&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;div class=&quot;text&quot;&gt;&lt;pre&gt;In the plain-MSD fit, the plateau B is the Li MSD (in Å², at that τ and T) that the fit says Li reach far from the dopant, once its suppression has died out. It is the system's own bulk-like mobility, read off the profile itself and not taken from the far-Li group.

The fitted profile is MSD(r) = B · (1 − A · f(r)). B is the level the curve levels off to at large r, and A is the fractional dip below it near the dopant. So the MSD at the dopant is B(1 − A), and the absolute loss in mobility is A·B (Å²).

**What it tells you**
- **Unperturbed mobility:** B is what Li do when the dopant no longer matters. Comparing B across elements (Al, Ga, Ca...) at the same T and τ shows whether the dopant also changes the overall mobility of the cell, not just the local dip. The ratio fit hides this, because dividing by MSD(far) forces its plateau to about 1.
- **Is the radius contained?** Compare B with `far_msd_A2` in the profile csv, which is the MSD of the Li farther than 7 Å (shown as the grey line on the plots).
  - B ≈ far MSD: the profile saturates inside the far edge, so the radius r95 is a real measurement.
  - B &amp;gt; far MSD: the MSD is still rising past the far edge. The influence extends beyond 7 Å, and r95 is a lower bound or an extrapolation. This is the same information as B &amp;gt; 1 in the ratio fit.
  - B &amp;lt; far MSD: the far group is faster than the outer part of the profile. This usually means the dopants are dense enough that no region is truly unperturbed, so the far-Li reference is not clean.
- **Equivalence with the ratio fit:** B / MSD(far) is the old ratio-fit plateau.

**Cautions**
- B is allowed between 0.5 and 3 times the mean of the outermost 3 fitted points. If B sits at a bound, the profile has not flattened in your r range and B is not a real plateau.
- B grows with τ and with T, so only compare it at the same T and τ.
- With dense doping, periodic images of the dopants overlap and the outer region never recovers. B then reports a perturbed level, not a pure-LLZO one. Only a pure (undoped) run can tell you whether B is the true bulk value.&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/section&gt;&lt;section class=&quot;msg user&quot;&gt;&lt;h3&gt;User&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;div class=&quot;text&quot;&gt;&lt;pre&gt;dude....i need one more plot, which has the radius, from sigmoid only, and not with *, means the ones which are correct and not artefacts, and dept of supresion, plateu, combines....over all temps, like the radius summary tau 20. but with all temps, and i want one where all temps and all times are included. so the only thing left to compare is the dopant. also, make the temp dependence plot a bit cleaner. i cant make out anything in it. present updated file&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/section&gt;&lt;section class=&quot;msg assistant&quot;&gt;&lt;h3&gt;Assistant&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;details class=&quot;thinking&quot;&gt;&lt;summary&gt;Thinking&lt;/summary&gt;&lt;pre&gt;

- build_doc.py&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/section&gt;&lt;section class=&quot;msg user&quot;&gt;&lt;h3&gt;User&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;div class=&quot;text&quot;&gt;&lt;pre&gt;okay .....i need one avrerage value per dopant, i can tell as a value for that dopant, as a radius of influence. gimme python command to compute from the csv which u have given.&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/section&gt;&lt;section class=&quot;msg assistant&quot;&gt;&lt;h3&gt;Assistant&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;div class=&quot;text&quot;&gt;&lt;pre&gt;Save this as `radius_avg.py`. It reads `radius_summary.csv`, keeps only the same valid fits as the plots, and gives one value per dopant:

```python
import sys, numpy as np, pandas as pd

csv = sys.argv[1]                          # radius/radius_summary.csv  or  radius_plainmsd/radius_summary.csv
taus = [float(x) for x in sys.argv[2:]]    # optional: only these tau (ps)

d = pd.read_csv(csv)
d = d[d.level == 'element']                # pooled-per-dopant rows, not single folders
cap = d[d.model == 'free'][['name', 'T', 'tau_ps', 'r_max_used']]            # measured range, stored on the 'free' row
s = d[d.model == 'sigmoid'].merge(cap, on=['name', 'T', 'tau_ps'])
s = s[(s.A &amp;gt;= 0.1) &amp;amp; (s.r95 &amp;gt; 0) &amp;amp; (s.r95 &amp;lt;= s.r_max_used)]                   # valid only: no '*', no noise-level depth
if taus:
    keep = np.zeros(len(s), bool)
    for t in taus: keep |= np.isclose(s.tau_ps, t, rtol=0.05)
    s = s[keep]

out = s.groupby('name').agg(n=('r95', 'size'), r95_mean=('r95', 'mean'), r95_std=('r95', 'std'),
                            r95_median=('r95', 'median'), A_mean=('A', 'mean'), B_mean=('B', 'mean'))
out['r95_sem'] = out.r95_std / np.sqrt(out.n)
print(out.round(2).to_string())
```

Run it with:

```
python radius_avg.py radius/radius_summary.csv                  # all T and all tau
python radius_avg.py radius/radius_summary.csv 20               # only tau = 20 ps
python radius_avg.py radius_plainmsd/radius_summary.csv 20 50
```

- **`r95_mean ± r95_std`:** quote this as the radius of influence for that dopant. The std shows how much it moves across T and τ. `n` is the number of valid (T, τ) fits that went into the average.
- **A and B:** `A_mean` is fine to average. `B_mean` is only meaningful in `radius/`, where B is dimensionless. In `radius_plainmsd/` B is in Å² and grows with T and τ, so skip it there or average it at a single T and τ.
- **Mixing lags:** if r95 grows with τ for your system, averaging over all lags blurs that, so use the τ filter and quote one lag. If r95 is flat across T and τ, the all-data average is fine.
- **Unequal `n`:** the average only uses valid fits. If one dopant has far fewer valid fits than the others, check `n` before comparing means.&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/section&gt;&lt;section class=&quot;msg user&quot;&gt;&lt;h3&gt;User&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;div class=&quot;text&quot;&gt;&lt;pre&gt;_debdipto/2x2x4-new/Li-substitution/radius$ python ../../pathway_analysis_scripts/with_flag_smoothed/radius_svg.py radius_summary.csv

Here `r95_std` is the spread of r95 across the valid (T, τ) fits, and `n` is how many fits went into the average.

It tells you how precisely the mean itself is known, not how much r95 varies. A small SEM means the average is well pinned down. It shrinks as you add more fits, even if the individual values scatter a lot.

**Which one to quote**
- To say &amp;quot;dopant X has a radius of influence of about 5.2 Å&amp;quot;, quote `r95_mean ± r95_std`. That shows the real spread of the radius across temperatures and lags.
- The SEM is only a good error bar if the fits are independent repeats of the same quantity. Yours are not. They are different T and τ values of the same trajectories, so they are correlated, and r95 may also genuinely change with T or τ. The SEM would then look too small and make the number seem more certain than it is.
- If you want a statistical error on the radius itself, use the bootstrap bars from the plots (`r95_lo`, `r95_hi` in the csv). Those come from resampling the time blocks.

So use `r95_std` as the uncertainty and treat `r95_sem` as a secondary number.&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/section&gt;&lt;section class=&quot;msg user&quot;&gt;&lt;h3&gt;User&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;div class=&quot;text&quot;&gt;&lt;pre&gt;_debdipto/2x2x4-new/Li-substitution/radius$ python ../../pathway_analysis_scripts/with_flag_smoothed/radius_svg.py radius_summary.csv

identical...how do u interpret this?...also more important.....what is the use of this parameter...the sphere of influence/&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/section&gt;&lt;section class=&quot;msg assistant&quot;&gt;&lt;h3&gt;Assistant&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;div class=&quot;text&quot;&gt;&lt;pre&gt;**Why the two outputs are identical**

This is expected, and I should have said so earlier. The plain fit is the ratio fit multiplied by one constant (the far-Li MSD), which is not a fitted quantity. Multiplying a curve by a constant leaves the radius and the depth unchanged. Only the plateau B changes units, from &amp;quot;×MSD(far)&amp;quot; to Å².

What the plain fit does give you is a check on the normalisation. B in the ratio run comes out at 0.99–1.02, so the profile's own outer plateau matches the far-Li MSD, and the far reference is consistent. The Å² values in the plain run (6.3–7.7) are just that same plateau averaged over different T and τ, so don't compare them across dopants. You can ignore the plain results for radius and depth.

**What the numbers say**
- **Mg is clearly the smallest and weakest:** about 4.0 Å radius and only a ~24% MSD dip, with a tight spread.
- **Al, Ga, Fe and Zn:** these sit at about 6 to 6.8 Å. Al (6.8 ± 0.7) and Ga (6.4 ± 0.6) overlap within their spread. Fe (6.0 ± 1.5) has a large spread, so its radius changes strongly with T or τ. Zn is 5.9 ± 0.3 with a shallow dip of 0.29.
- **Depth does separate them:** Al has the deepest suppression (0.54), then Ga (0.48), Fe (0.37), Zn (0.29) and Mg (0.24).

**Two cautions**
1. **r95 is biased low for shallow dips.** It is the distance where the deficit falls to 5% of the plateau. For a shallow dip like Mg's, 5% is a large fraction of the dip, so the radius comes out shorter by construction. Use r50 (half-recovery) when you rank dopants, since it doesn't depend on depth. It is in the same csv, so you can run the same script with `r50` swapped in.
2. **Al, Ga and Fe have r95 near the 7 Å far edge.** The reference Li then partly overlap the perturbed zone. B ≈ 1.0 says this isn't a big problem, but it is the reason to be careful with those three.

**What the radius of influence is for**

It is the distance over which a dopant changes the mobility of nearby Li. It is useful in four ways:
- **Overlap and concentration:** the dopant's affected volume is about (4/3)πr³. When the number of dopants times that volume approaches your cell volume, the affected zones overlap and almost no Li moves freely. This predicts the doping level where conductivity peaks and then falls, which is the main practical use.
- **Ranking dopants:** radius and depth together say how many Li each dopant slows and by how much. A large, deep dopant like Al harms conductivity at lower concentration than a small, shallow one like Mg.
- **Shells and cell size:** it justifies your shell edges and far cutoff. Anything beyond the radius is bulk-like, and the box should be larger than twice the radius, or periodic images of the dopant overlap.
- **Linking to mechanism:** it tells you whether the effect is short-range (local site blocking) or long-range (strain or electrostatics), which you can compare with your hop and concerted-motion results.

For a single &amp;quot;cost per dopant&amp;quot; number, the csv also has `V_A3` and `r_eff` (the model-free frozen-sphere equivalent). That combines radius and depth, so it may suit the overlap argument better than r95 alone.&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/section&gt;&lt;section class=&quot;msg user&quot;&gt;&lt;h3&gt;User&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;div class=&quot;text&quot;&gt;&lt;pre&gt;gimem updated code to print both....also iw anna verify the relation i though about the radius with slater effective charge. values are as so:

Al3+ - 8.85, Fe3+ - 6.6 Ga3+ - 9.85 Mg2+ - 7.85 Zn2+ - 8.85 

verify these values also&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/section&gt;&lt;section class=&quot;msg assistant&quot;&gt;&lt;h3&gt;Assistant&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;div class=&quot;text&quot;&gt;&lt;pre&gt;Your Slater values are all correct, but with your r95 numbers Z_eff does not predict the radius of influence. I read &amp;quot;print both&amp;quot; as r95 and r50, so `radius_avg.py` now prints both, with mean, std, median and SEM for each. I only ran it on a made-up csv, not your real one, so run it on `radius_summary.csv` yourself. It also prints a Z_eff correlation block, which for the two numbers I could check (below) gave weak, non-significant results.

**Slater values**

The rule is Z_eff = Z − S for the outermost electron of the ion. S is 0.35 per other electron in the same group and 0.85 or 1.00 for the shells below, and 1.00 for everything below a d electron.

| Ion | Config | Outermost e⁻ | S | Z_eff |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Al³⁺ (Z=13) | 1s² 2s² 2p⁶ | 2p | 7×0.35 + 2×0.85 = 4.15 | 8.85 ✓ |
| Mg²⁺ (Z=12) | 1s² 2s² 2p⁶ | 2p | 4.15 | 7.85 ✓ |
| Zn²⁺ (Z=30) | [Ar] 3d¹⁰ | 3d | 9×0.35 + 18 = 21.15 | 8.85 ✓ |
| Ga³⁺ (Z=31) | [Ar] 3d¹⁰ | 3d | 21.15 | 9.85 ✓ |
| Fe³⁺ (Z=26) | [Ar] 3d⁵ | 3d | 4×0.35 + 18 = 19.40 | 6.60 ✓ |

For Zn²⁺ and Ga³⁺ the value depends on which electron you take as the outermost. I used 3d, the highest occupied level. If you used the 3p electron instead you would get about 18.75 and 19.75, so state your choice when you write it up.

**Does Z_eff track the radius?**

You didn't say which relation you expected, so I tested Z_eff against your r95 and depth values, and I haven't tested r50 yet:
- **r95:** Pearson +0.39 (p = 0.52), Spearman +0.46 (p = 0.43).
- **Depth A:** Pearson +0.44 (p = 0.46), Spearman +0.46 (p = 0.43).

With only five dopants these can't be called significant. The values also show why: Al and Zn have the same Z_eff (8.85) but r95 of 6.8 and 5.9 Å and depths of 0.54 and 0.29. Mg has a middle Z_eff but the smallest radius.

**A stronger factor: the charge of the dopant**

All the dopants sit on Li sites, so the defect carries an excess charge of +2 for the 3+ ions (Al, Ga, Fe) and +1 for the 2+ ions (Mg, Zn). That grouping fits your data better:
- **Charge vs r95:** Spearman +0.87 (p ≈ 0.06), Pearson +0.74.
- **Charge vs depth A:** Pearson +0.86, Spearman +0.87 (p ≈ 0.06).

This is suggestive, not proven, at n = 5. Z_eff, being an atomic-orbital property, probably explains little beyond that, but you could check ionic radius or size mismatch as another variable. Zn stands out as the 2+ ion with a fairly large r95 (5.9 Å), so check whether it is a real outlier once you use r50.

Files are in /home/claude/outputs:
- radius_avg.py&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/section&gt;&lt;section class=&quot;msg user&quot;&gt;&lt;h3&gt;User&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;div class=&quot;text&quot;&gt;&lt;pre&gt;is it useful...this parameter...and ha anyone published this?&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/section&gt;&lt;section class=&quot;msg assistant&quot;&gt;&lt;h3&gt;Assistant&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;details class=&quot;thinking&quot;&gt;&lt;summary&gt;Thinking&lt;/summary&gt;&lt;pre&gt;





&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/details&gt;&lt;div class=&quot;text&quot;&gt;&lt;pre&gt;**Is it useful?** Yes, with limits.

**What it's good for**
- **Comparison:** it turns &amp;quot;dopant X slows Li down&amp;quot; into a distance and a depth you can compare across dopants and temperatures.
- **Overlap argument:** it gives you a way to predict at what doping level the affected zones start to overlap.
- **Cell setup:** it tells you how big the box and the far reference region need to be.

**Limits**
- **It depends on how it's defined:** r95 depends on the 5% cutoff, the sigmoid shape, T and τ, which is why r50 is the safer number to compare.
- **Small sample:** you have five dopants, so any trend against an atomic property (like Z_eff) is weak.
- **The profile is the result:** the MSD(r)/MSD(far) curve with its control band is more trustworthy than any single radius, so show it next to the number.
- **A direct test of whether it is intrinsic:** if the radius stays about the same when you go from 1 to 3 dopants in the cell, it is a property of the dopant and not of the box.

**Has anyone published this?** I found no paper that defines a distance-resolved radius of influence on Li dynamics in garnets. That is not proof that none exists. My searches were short, and I couldn't read one relevant paper because the site blocked me.

The closest work I found:
- [Gao, Jalem and Tateyama, J. Mater. Chem. A 2022](https://pubs.rsc.org/en/content/articlehtml/2022/ta/d2ta00545j) uses first-principles MD at LLZO grain boundaries. It shows Al fragmenting the Li diffusion network near its sites, but it gives no distance or radius.
- [Fu, Bulbule and Jin, 2026](https://pubs.rsc.org/en/content/articlehtml/2026/ta/d5ta10347a) studies the same dopants as you (Al, Fe, Ga, Zn). It describes vacancies &amp;quot;influenced&amp;quot; by an adjacent dopant versus &amp;quot;active&amp;quot; vacancies, but only qualitatively, with no distance cutoff and no spatial measurement. It looks like a natural paper to compare against.
- [Clarifying the Dopant Local Structure...](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/PMC10687891/) turned up in my searches, but I couldn't open it, so I can't say what it measures.

The qualitative idea of dopant-affected Li appears in the literature. What I didn't find is a statistically controlled version of it, with a null control, bootstrap error bars, a far-Li normaliser and a fitted radius. If you plan to claim novelty, search Google Scholar and Web of Science with terms like &amp;quot;dopant-affected zone&amp;quot;, &amp;quot;blocking radius&amp;quot; and &amp;quot;spatially resolved Li MSD&amp;quot; before you write it up.

Sources:
- [Atomistic insight into the dopant impacts at the garnet Li7La3Zr2O12 solid electrolyte grain boundaries](https://pubs.rsc.org/en/content/articlehtml/2022/ta/d2ta00545j)
- [Influence of Li-site dopants on phase stabilization, lithium distribution, and ionic conductivity in high-entropy Li-garnet solid electrolytes](https://pubs.rsc.org/en/content/articlehtml/2026/ta/d5ta10347a)
- [Clarifying the Dopant Local Structure and Effect on Ionic Conductivity in Garnet Solid-State Electrolytes](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/PMC10687891/)&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/section&gt;&lt;section class=&quot;msg user&quot;&gt;&lt;h3&gt;User&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;div class=&quot;text&quot;&gt;&lt;pre&gt;gimme a prompt i can give claude code...so i t can definitively tel me if smtg like this exists.&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/section&gt;&lt;section class=&quot;msg assistant&quot;&gt;&lt;h3&gt;Assistant&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;div class=&quot;text&quot;&gt;&lt;pre&gt;Paste this into Claude Code:

```
TASK: Literature novelty check. Determine, as rigorously as you can, whether the analysis described below has already been published. Do NOT write any code. Research only. Be honest: a search can never prove absence, so report what you searched, what you found and what you could not access.

THE ANALYSIS I WANT TO CHECK
In MD simulations of Li-substituted garnet solid electrolytes (LLZO, Li7La3Zr2O12, doped on the Li site with Al3+, Ga3+, Fe3+, Mg2+, Zn2+), I compute a &amp;quot;radius of influence&amp;quot; of each dopant on Li mobility:
- Every Li at every origin time t0 gets its distance r to the nearest dopant. Its MSD over a lag tau is plotted as a continuous function MSD(r), using Gaussian-kernel regression (no distance shells).
- The profile is normalised by the MSD of far Li (farther than about 7 A from every dopant): y(r) = MSD(r) / MSD(far).
- A control profile is computed around non-dopant Li-site reference points, which gives a null band.
- Uncertainty comes from a block bootstrap over time blocks.
- y(r) is fitted with a sigmoid (also exponential and Gaussian) with a free plateau. This gives the depth of suppression A and a radius r95 (the distance where the deficit has recovered to 95%), plus r50. A model-free &amp;quot;effective frozen-sphere radius&amp;quot; is also computed.
- The radius is compared across dopants, temperatures and lag times, and I want to relate it to dopant charge, ionic radius and Slater effective charge.

WHAT COUNTS AS &amp;quot;ALREADY EXISTS&amp;quot; (rate each paper against these, separately)
 (a) Any paper that measures Li dynamics (MSD, diffusion, hop rate, residence time, or similar) as a function of DISTANCE from a dopant, defect or substituent in a solid electrolyte, for any dopant or defect, in any material class (garnets first, then NASICON, argyrodites, perovskites LLTO, sulfides, halides, antiperovskites).
 (b) Any paper that defines a numerical &amp;quot;radius of influence&amp;quot;, &amp;quot;affected zone&amp;quot;, &amp;quot;blocking zone&amp;quot;, &amp;quot;trapping radius&amp;quot;, &amp;quot;dopant-vacancy association radius&amp;quot;, &amp;quot;defect interaction range&amp;quot; or similar for dopants on ionic transport.
 (c) Any paper that uses such a radius to predict the optimal doping concentration through overlap or percolation arguments.
 (d) The same methodology applied to other mobile ions (O2-, Na+, H+) in oxides such as doped ceria, zirconia or perovskites. Analogies count as related work, but flag them as another material.

SEARCH STRATEGY (do all of it)
1. Run at least 30 distinct searches covering: Google Scholar, Web of Science, Scopus, arXiv, ChemRxiv, Semantic Scholar, OpenAlex, Crossref, and publisher sites (RSC, ACS, Elsevier, Wiley, Nature, APS, IOP). Use search operators and varied vocabulary, for example:
   &amp;quot;radius of influence&amp;quot; dopant Li diffusion; &amp;quot;dopant-affected&amp;quot; OR &amp;quot;blocking zone&amp;quot; OR &amp;quot;trapping zone&amp;quot; garnet; spatially resolved Li mobility distance from dopant; Li MSD vs distance to dopant molecular dynamics; local Li dynamics around aliovalent dopant solid electrolyte; dopant-induced Li immobilization length scale; defect cluster size / correlation length ionic conductivity; &amp;quot;percolation&amp;quot; &amp;quot;excluded volume&amp;quot; dopant LLZO; Li vacancy trapping near Al/Ga LLZO; ab initio MD / machine-learning MD LLZO Ga Al Fe Zn Mg local diffusion; and the same for NASICON, LLTO, argyrodite, ceria, YSZ.
2. Do forward and backward citation chasing from the most relevant papers: follow what they cite and what cites them (Google Scholar &amp;quot;Cited by&amp;quot;, OpenAlex, Semantic Scholar).
3. Include these known starting points and read what they actually contain:
   - Gao, Jalem, Tateyama, &amp;quot;Atomistic insight into the dopant impacts at the garnet LLZO solid electrolyte grain boundaries&amp;quot;, J. Mater. Chem. A 2022 (doi 10.1039/d2ta00545j)
   - Fu, Bulbule, Jin, &amp;quot;Influence of Li-site dopants on phase stabilization, lithium distribution, and ionic conductivity in high-entropy Li-garnet solid electrolytes&amp;quot;, J. Mater. Chem. A 2026 (doi 10.1039/d5ta10347a), and the earlier works it cites for &amp;quot;influenced&amp;quot; vs &amp;quot;active&amp;quot; vacancies (Chen et al., Thompson et al.)
   - &amp;quot;Clarifying the Dopant Local Structure and Effect on Ionic Conductivity in Garnet Solid-State Electrolytes for Lithium-Ion Batteries&amp;quot; (PMC10687891)
   - arXiv 1605.08598 (network-blocking substitutions in LLZO)
4. Read the full text or at least the methods and results of the 15 most relevant papers. Do not rely on abstracts or titles. If a paper is paywalled or blocked, say so and mark it &amp;quot;unverified, not read&amp;quot;.
5. Search again after reading, using the vocabulary the papers themselves use.

HARD RULES
- Do not invent papers, authors, DOIs or numbers. Every claim must have a URL or DOI that you actually opened. If you did not open it, mark it &amp;quot;not read&amp;quot;.
- Do not quote more than a short phrase from any paper. Paraphrase instead.
- Distinguish clearly between &amp;quot;found nothing&amp;quot; and &amp;quot;found it does not exist&amp;quot;. You cannot prove the latter.

DELIVERABLE (one markdown report, saved as novelty_check.md in the working directory)
1. Verdict in 3 sentences: for each of (a), (b), (c) and (d), is there prior work, partial prior work or nothing found?
2. A table of the 10 to 20 closest papers: citation, DOI/URL, material, dopants, what is measured, whether it is distance-resolved (yes/no), whether it gives a numerical radius (yes/no), whether it uses the radius for concentration prediction (yes/no), and whether you read the full text.
3. For the single closest paper, a paragraph on exactly what it does and does not do compared with my method (kernel regression of MSD vs distance, far normalisation, null control, bootstrap, fitted radius).
4. Which individual ingredients of my method have precedent (for example kernel regression of a property vs distance, null-control profiles, block bootstrap, effective frozen-sphere volume) even if the combination has none.
5. A list of every search you ran (query and database) and every paper you could not access, so I can repeat or extend it.
6. A final confidence statement: how sure you are that nothing identical exists, and what would raise that confidence (for example specific researchers or groups to check).
```

&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/details&gt;&lt;/section&gt;&lt;section class=&quot;msg assistant&quot;&gt;&lt;h3&gt;Assistant&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;div class=&quot;text&quot;&gt;&lt;pre&gt;I'll write this as a standalone script (numpy and scipy only), since I don't have your `llzo_sites` module here. Then I'll test it on your CIF.&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/section&gt;&lt;section class=&quot;msg user&quot;&gt;&lt;h3&gt;User&lt;/h3&gt;&lt;div class=&quot;text&quot;&gt;&lt;pre&gt;here&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;details class=&quot;attachment&quot;&gt;&lt;summary&gt;Attachment: 02_compare_temps.py&lt;/summary&gt;&lt;pre&gt;#!/usr/bin/env python3
&amp;quot;&amp;quot;&amp;quot;
02_compare_temps.py - compare the Li free-energy maps of ONE system across temperatures.

Reads results/&amp;lt;system&amp;gt;/density_F_T*K.npz + density_summary_T*K.json written by 01_density_free_energy.py
(no dumps needed). Same layout as the single-T plots (full-box min-F projections along z, y, x), one row per
temperature, one common colour scale.

Two views of the same data (--units both, default):
  eV : F - F_site (eV)         - if the free-energy LANDSCAPE U(r) is temperature independent this does not change
  kT : (F - F_site) / kT       - if the DENSITY RATIO rho(r)/rho_site is temperature independent (e.g. the values
                                 are set by geometry/smoothing tails, not by an energy) this does not change
Look at where the difference maps are ~0 in each view: that tells you which of the two is true, region by region.

Alignment (important for difference maps): all temperatures use ONE shift, computed from the reference
temperature (--ref-T, default lowest), so maps stay aligned voxel-for-voxel.
  --shift SX SY SZ            fixed periodic shift in A
  --center Ga | Ga1 | all     one set of figures per dopant atom, that atom at the box centre IN EVERY ROW: the SAME fixed shift for
                              every temperature = the reference T's (--ref-T) mean dopant position; the centre never moves
  --slab W                    with --center: min-project only a +/-W A slab through that atom

Outputs (results/&amp;lt;system&amp;gt;/):
  compare_T[_&amp;lt;label&amp;gt;].png, compare_T_kT[_&amp;lt;label&amp;gt;].png     F_rel per temperature
  diff_T[_&amp;lt;label&amp;gt;].png,    diff_T_kT[_&amp;lt;label&amp;gt;].png        difference from the reference temperature
  dopant_rdf_T.png                                         Li-dopant g(r) at every T
  compare_T_table.csv, compare_T_dopants.csv               numbers (also printed)

usage:
  python 02_compare_temps.py results/MD_run_1Ga_1Ru
  python 02_compare_temps.py results/MD_run_1Ga_1Ru --center all --ref-T 800
&amp;quot;&amp;quot;&amp;quot;
import argparse

KB = 8.617333262e-5


def load(folder):
    runs = {}
    for p in glob.glob(os.path.join(folder, &amp;quot;density_F_T*K.npz&amp;quot;)):
        T = int(re.search(r&amp;quot;T(\d+)K&amp;quot;, p).group(1))
        z = np.load(p)
        js = os.path.join(folder, f&amp;quot;density_summary_T{T}K.json&amp;quot;)
        if &amp;quot;F_rel&amp;quot; not in z.files or not os.path.exists(js):
            raise SystemExit(f&amp;quot;{p}: written by an old 01 script (no F_rel / summary). Rerun 01_density_free_energy.py.&amp;quot;)
        runs[T] = dict(F=z[&amp;quot;F_rel&amp;quot;], rho=z[&amp;quot;rho&amp;quot;], L=z[&amp;quot;L&amp;quot;], rho_uni=float(z[&amp;quot;rho_uniform&amp;quot;]),
                       summ=json.load(open(js)), rdf_r=z[&amp;quot;rdf_r&amp;quot;] if &amp;quot;rdf_r&amp;quot; in z.files else None,
                       rdf={k[6:]: z[k] for k in z.files if k.startswith(&amp;quot;rdf_g_&amp;quot;)})
    if not runs:
        raise SystemExit(f&amp;quot;no density_F_T*K.npz in {folder}&amp;quot;)
    return dict(sorted(runs.items()))


def main():
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__, formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter)
    ap.add_argument(&amp;quot;folder&amp;quot;, help=&amp;quot;results/&amp;lt;system&amp;gt; folder from 01_density_free_energy.py&amp;quot;)
    ap.add_argument(&amp;quot;--units&amp;quot;, choices=[&amp;quot;both&amp;quot;, &amp;quot;eV&amp;quot;, &amp;quot;kT&amp;quot;], default=&amp;quot;both&amp;quot;)
    ap.add_argument(&amp;quot;--shift&amp;quot;, type=float, nargs=3, default=[0, 0, 0], metavar=(&amp;quot;SX&amp;quot;, &amp;quot;SY&amp;quot;, &amp;quot;SZ&amp;quot;))
    ap.add_argument(&amp;quot;--center&amp;quot;, help=&amp;quot;dopant element (Ga), atom label (Ga1) or 'all'&amp;quot;)
    ap.add_argument(&amp;quot;--slab&amp;quot;, type=float, default=0.0, help=&amp;quot;with --center: half-thickness (A) of a slab projection&amp;quot;)
    ap.add_argument(&amp;quot;--ref-T&amp;quot;, type=int, help=&amp;quot;reference temperature (difference maps, centring); default lowest&amp;quot;)
    ap.add_argument(&amp;quot;--vmax&amp;quot;, type=float, default=0.6, help=&amp;quot;eV colour maximum&amp;quot;)
    ap.add_argument(&amp;quot;--vmax-kT&amp;quot;, type=float, default=6.0, help=&amp;quot;kT colour maximum&amp;quot;)
    ap.add_argument(&amp;quot;--dmax&amp;quot;, type=float, default=0.15, help=&amp;quot;+/- range of eV difference maps&amp;quot;)
    ap.add_argument(&amp;quot;--dmax-kT&amp;quot;, type=float, default=1.5, help=&amp;quot;+/- range of kT difference maps&amp;quot;)
    args = ap.parse_args()
    if args.slab &amp;gt; 0 and not args.center:
        ap.error(&amp;quot;--slab needs --center&amp;quot;)

    import matplotlib
    matplotlib.use(&amp;quot;Agg&amp;quot;)
    import matplotlib.pyplot as plt

    runs = load(args.folder)
    Ts = list(runs)
    T0 = args.ref_T or Ts[0]
    ref = runs[T0]
    L = ref[&amp;quot;L&amp;quot;]
    dops0 = ref[&amp;quot;summ&amp;quot;][&amp;quot;dopants&amp;quot;]
    print(f&amp;quot;temperatures: {Ts}   reference: {T0} K&amp;quot;)

    # ---- views (label, shift)
    views = [(None, np.array(args.shift, float))]
    if args.center:
        if not dops0:
            print(&amp;quot;[center] --center ignored: no dopant atoms in this system; plotting the unshifted maps&amp;quot;)
        else:
            pick = [d[&amp;quot;label&amp;quot;] for d in dops0 if args.center in (d[&amp;quot;label&amp;quot;], d[&amp;quot;element&amp;quot;]) or args.center == &amp;quot;all&amp;quot;]
            if not pick:
                print(f&amp;quot;[center] --center {args.center} ignored: no such dopant. Available: {[d['label'] for d in dops0]}; &amp;quot;
                      &amp;quot;plotting the unshifted maps&amp;quot;)
            else:
                views = [(lab, None) for lab in pick]
    units = [&amp;quot;eV&amp;quot;, &amp;quot;kT&amp;quot;] if args.units == &amp;quot;both&amp;quot; else [args.units]

    CM_F = plt.get_cmap(&amp;quot;viridis_r&amp;quot;).copy()
    CM_F.set_bad(&amp;quot;0.82&amp;quot;)
    CM_D = plt.get_cmap(&amp;quot;RdBu_r&amp;quot;).copy()
    CM_D.set_bad(&amp;quot;0.82&amp;quot;)
    DCOL, PAL = {}, [&amp;quot;red&amp;quot;, &amp;quot;white&amp;quot;, &amp;quot;orange&amp;quot;, &amp;quot;cyan&amp;quot;, &amp;quot;magenta&amp;quot;]

    def scaled(T, unit):
        F = runs[T][&amp;quot;F&amp;quot;]
        return F / (KB * T) if unit == &amp;quot;kT&amp;quot; else F

    def ref_shift(lab):
        &amp;quot;&amp;quot;&amp;quot;ONE fixed shift for every temperature: the dopant's mean position at the reference T (--ref-T) goes to the box centre.
        The centre never moves between rows, so T and T0 stay aligned voxel-for-voxel (the lattice is common to all T).&amp;quot;&amp;quot;&amp;quot;
        d = next(d_ for d_ in runs[T0][&amp;quot;summ&amp;quot;][&amp;quot;dopants&amp;quot;] if d_[&amp;quot;label&amp;quot;] == lab)
        return L / 2 - np.array(d[&amp;quot;pos_A&amp;quot;])

    def draw(kind, unit, lab, shift):
        n_r = len(Ts)
        fig, axs = plt.subplots(n_r, 3, figsize=(11, 3.3 * n_r), dpi=150, squeeze=False)
        if unit == &amp;quot;eV&amp;quot;:
            fr, dr, lbl = (-0.1, args.vmax), args.dmax, &amp;quot;eV&amp;quot;
        else:
            fr, dr, lbl = (-1.0, args.vmax_kT), args.dmax_kT, &amp;quot;kT&amp;quot;
        for ri, T in enumerate(Ts):
            shT = shR = shift if lab is None else ref_shift(lab)
            F, sh = mp.apply_shift(scaled(T, unit), L, shT)
            Fr, _ = mp.apply_shift(scaled(T0, unit), L, shR)
            for ci, (axis, xl, yl, ix, iy) in enumerate(mp.PANELS):
                ax = axs[ri, ci]
                pj = mp.project(F, axis, args.slab if lab else 0.0, L)
                if kind == &amp;quot;F&amp;quot;:
                    proj, cmap, vmin, vmax = pj, CM_F, fr[0], fr[1]
                else:
                    proj, cmap, vmin, vmax = pj - mp.project(Fr, axis, args.slab if lab else 0.0, L), CM_D, -dr, dr
                im = ax.imshow(np.ma.masked_invalid(proj).T, origin=&amp;quot;lower&amp;quot;, extent=[0, L[ix], 0, L[iy]], cmap=cmap,
                               vmin=vmin, vmax=vmax, aspect=&amp;quot;equal&amp;quot;)
                for d in runs[T][&amp;quot;summ&amp;quot;][&amp;quot;dopants&amp;quot;]:
                    p = (np.array(d[&amp;quot;pos_A&amp;quot;]) + sh) % L
                    if args.slab &amp;gt; 0 and lab and abs(p[axis] - L[axis] / 2) &amp;gt; args.slab + 1.0:
                        continue
                    ax.scatter(p[ix], p[iy], s=90 if d[&amp;quot;label&amp;quot;] == lab else 45, marker=&amp;quot;*&amp;quot;,
                               color=DCOL.setdefault(d[&amp;quot;element&amp;quot;], PAL[len(DCOL) % len(PAL)]), edgecolor=&amp;quot;k&amp;quot;,
                               linewidth=0.8 if d[&amp;quot;label&amp;quot;] == lab else 0.5, zorder=5)
                ax.set_ylabel((f&amp;quot;{T} K\n&amp;quot; if ci == 0 else &amp;quot;&amp;quot;) + f&amp;quot;{yl} (A)&amp;quot;)
                ax.set_xlabel(f&amp;quot;{xl} (A)&amp;quot;)
                if ri == 0:
                    ax.set_title(f&amp;quot;min-F in +/-{args.slab:g} A slab normal to {'xyz'[axis]}&amp;quot; if (args.slab &amp;gt; 0 and lab)
                                 else f&amp;quot;min-F projection along {'xyz'[axis]}&amp;quot;, fontsize=10)
            fig.colorbar(im, ax=axs[ri, :], shrink=0.9, pad=0.02,
                         label=(f&amp;quot;(F - F_site) / kT&amp;quot; if unit == &amp;quot;kT&amp;quot; else &amp;quot;F - F_site (eV)&amp;quot;) if kind == &amp;quot;F&amp;quot;
                         else f&amp;quot;delta vs {T0} K ({lbl})&amp;quot;)
        what = &amp;quot;F - F_site&amp;quot; if unit == &amp;quot;eV&amp;quot; else &amp;quot;(F - F_site)/kT&amp;quot;
        fig.suptitle(f&amp;quot;{os.path.basename(os.path.normpath(args.folder))}: &amp;quot;
                     + (f&amp;quot;{what} (grey = unsampled)&amp;quot; if kind == &amp;quot;F&amp;quot;
                        else f&amp;quot;{what}, difference from {T0} K (grey = unsampled at either T)&amp;quot;)
                     + (f&amp;quot;, centred on {lab}&amp;quot; if lab else (f&amp;quot;, shift {list(shift)} A&amp;quot; if np.any(shift) else &amp;quot;&amp;quot;)),
                     y=0.995)
        name = (&amp;quot;compare_T&amp;quot; if kind == &amp;quot;F&amp;quot; else &amp;quot;diff_T&amp;quot;) + (&amp;quot;_kT&amp;quot; if unit == &amp;quot;kT&amp;quot; else &amp;quot;&amp;quot;) + (f&amp;quot;_{lab}&amp;quot; if lab else &amp;quot;&amp;quot;)
        out = os.path.join(args.folder, name + &amp;quot;.png&amp;quot;)
        fig.savefig(out, bbox_inches=&amp;quot;tight&amp;quot;)
        plt.close(fig)
        print(&amp;quot;[+]&amp;quot;, out)

    for lab, shift in views:
        for unit in units:
            draw(&amp;quot;F&amp;quot;, unit, lab, shift)
            draw(&amp;quot;D&amp;quot;, unit, lab, shift)

    # ---- Li-dopant g(r) at every T
    labels = [d[&amp;quot;label&amp;quot;] for d in dops0]
    if labels and ref[&amp;quot;rdf_r&amp;quot;] is not None:
        fig, axs = plt.subplots(1, len(labels), figsize=(4.6 * len(labels), 3.8), dpi=150, squeeze=False)
        cm = plt.cm.plasma(np.linspace(0.05, 0.85, len(Ts)))
        for a, lab in zip(axs[0], labels):
            for c, T in zip(cm, Ts):
                if lab in runs[T][&amp;quot;rdf&amp;quot;]:
                    a.plot(runs[T][&amp;quot;rdf_r&amp;quot;], runs[T][&amp;quot;rdf&amp;quot;][lab], color=c, label=f&amp;quot;{T} K&amp;quot;)
            a.axhline(1, color=&amp;quot;0.6&amp;quot;, lw=0.6)
            a.set_title(lab)
            a.set_xlabel(&amp;quot;r (A)&amp;quot;)
            a.set_ylabel(&amp;quot;g_Li-dopant(r)&amp;quot;)
        axs[0][0].legend(fontsize=8)
        fig.tight_layout()
        out = os.path.join(args.folder, &amp;quot;dopant_rdf_T.png&amp;quot;)
        fig.savefig(out)
        plt.close(fig)
        print(&amp;quot;[+]&amp;quot;, out)

    # ---- tables
    rows, drows = [], []
    for T, r in runs.items():
        F, s = r[&amp;quot;F&amp;quot;], r[&amp;quot;summ&amp;quot;]
        kT = KB * T
        row = dict(T=T, F_site_over_kT=s[&amp;quot;F_site_ref_eV&amp;quot;] / kT, rho_site_over_uniform=float(np.exp(-s[&amp;quot;F_site_ref_eV&amp;quot;] / kT)),
                   V_eff_frac=float(np.exp(s[&amp;quot;F_site_ref_eV&amp;quot;] / kT)), sampled_frac=s[&amp;quot;sampled_fraction&amp;quot;],
                   max_resolvable_dF_eV=s[&amp;quot;max_resolvable_dF_eV&amp;quot;], max_resolvable_dF_kT=s[&amp;quot;max_resolvable_dF_eV&amp;quot;] / kT,
                   unsampled_below_pct_of_site=100.0 * float(np.exp(-s[&amp;quot;max_resolvable_dF_eV&amp;quot;] / kT)),
                   min_neff=s.get(&amp;quot;min_neff&amp;quot;, np.nan),
                   F_err_median_eV=s.get(&amp;quot;F_err_median_network_eV&amp;quot;, np.nan),
                   smoothing_sensitivity_eV=s.get(&amp;quot;smoothing_sensitivity_eV&amp;quot;, np.nan))
        for x in (0.1, 0.2, 0.3):
            row[f&amp;quot;frac_F_below_{x:g}eV&amp;quot;] = float((F &amp;lt; x).mean())
        for x in (1, 2, 3, 4):
            row[f&amp;quot;frac_F_below_{x}kT&amp;quot;] = float((F / kT &amp;lt; x).mean())
        rows.append(row)
        for d in s[&amp;quot;dopants&amp;quot;]:
            drows.append(dict(T=T, label=d[&amp;quot;label&amp;quot;], closest_Li_A=d[&amp;quot;closest_Li_A&amp;quot;], Li_within_2A=d[&amp;quot;mean_Li_within_2A&amp;quot;],
                              r_sphere_A=d[&amp;quot;r_sphere&amp;quot;], rho_over_uni_sphere=d[&amp;quot;rho_over_uni_sphere&amp;quot;],
                              control_median=d[&amp;quot;control_median&amp;quot;], control_p16=d[&amp;quot;control_p16&amp;quot;],
                              control_p84=d[&amp;quot;control_p84&amp;quot;],
                              ratio_to_control=d[&amp;quot;rho_over_uni_sphere&amp;quot;] / d[&amp;quot;control_median&amp;quot;] if d[&amp;quot;control_median&amp;quot;] else np.nan,
                              unsampled_frac_in_sphere=d[&amp;quot;unsampled_frac_in_sphere&amp;quot;], rms_disp_A=d[&amp;quot;rms_disp_A&amp;quot;]))
    for name, data in ((&amp;quot;compare_T_table.csv&amp;quot;, rows), (&amp;quot;compare_T_dopants.csv&amp;quot;, drows)):
        if data:
            with open(os.path.join(args.folder, name), &amp;quot;w&amp;quot;, newline=&amp;quot;&amp;quot;) as f:
                w = csv.DictWriter(f, fieldnames=list(data[0].keys()))
                w.writeheader()
                w.writerows(data)
            print(&amp;quot;\n&amp;quot; + name)
            print(&amp;quot;  &amp;quot;.join(f&amp;quot;{k[:19]:&amp;gt;19}&amp;quot; for k in data[0]))
            for row in data:
                print(&amp;quot;  &amp;quot;.join(f&amp;quot;{v:19.3f}&amp;quot; if isinstance(v, float) else f&amp;quot;{str(v):&amp;gt;19}&amp;quot; for v in row.values()))
    print(&amp;quot;\nSampling limit: a voxel is 'sampled' if the sigma-kernel holds &amp;gt;= min_neff raw counts. F_thr - F_site = &amp;quot;
          &amp;quot;max_resolvable_dF_kT (column) = ln(rho_site / rho_thr); unsampled_below_pct_of_site = 100 exp(-that) is the &amp;quot;
          &amp;quot;Li density (% of a site) below which voxels are grey. Values near this limit are detection-limit, not barriers.&amp;quot;)
    print(&amp;quot;\nNotes: grey/NaN voxels were not sampled: any barrier there only exceeds max_resolvable_dF (~5 kT, which &amp;quot;
          &amp;quot;grows with T in eV). Compare eV-differences (landscape) with kT-differences (density ratio); a real &amp;quot;
          &amp;quot;energy barrier is flat in eV, a geometric/smoothing tail is flat in kT. ratio_to_control &amp;lt; 1: Li depleted &amp;quot;
          &amp;quot;around the dopant relative to ordinary Li sites; closest_Li_A / Li_within_2A come from the raw dump.&amp;quot;)


if __name__ == &amp;quot;__main__&amp;quot;:
    warnings.filterwarnings(&amp;quot;ignore&amp;quot;, category=RuntimeWarning)
    main()
&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/details&gt;&lt;details class=&quot;attachment&quot;&gt;&lt;summary&gt;Attachment: 05_msd_by_shell.py&lt;/summary&gt;&lt;pre&gt;#!/usr/bin/env python3
&amp;quot;&amp;quot;&amp;quot;
05_msd_by_shell.py - does a dopant speed up or slow down nearby Li?  NO sites, NO hop definition.

For every Li at every time origin t0 the distance d0 to the dopant (minimum image, dopant position at t0) puts it into
a shell (--shells, default 3 5 7 A -&amp;gt; 0-3, 3-5, 5-7, &amp;gt;7; shells, far reference and error bars are the same in 04, 05, 06:
llzo_io.py).  Reference = each dopant alone and 'any' (nearest dopant); far = &amp;gt;7 A from EVERY dopant (--far self: from that
dopant only). Then
    MSD_shell(tau) = &amp;lt; |r(t0 + tau) - r(t0)|^2 &amp;gt;   over all Li and origins of that shell
(unwrapped positions, framework-COM drift removed, same as 00-04). The number to read is
    ratio(tau) = MSD_shell(tau) / MSD_far(tau)          &amp;lt; 1 : Li starting near the dopant move less (dopant slows)
                                                        &amp;gt; 1 : they move more
where 'far' is the outermost shell. Also printed: density ratio = mean Li count in the shell / count expected for a
uniform Li density in the same region (&amp;lt; 1 : Li depleted around the dopant; volumes from a grid, so overlap of shells of
several dopants and the far exclusion are handled).

Why this settles the site-based conflict: 04 counts hops between sites; near a dopant the ideal sites fit badly
(rattling counted as hops) and peak sites have very few hops. MSD needs neither.

Error bars: time origins are split into --blocks contiguous blocks; +/- = delete-one-block jackknife (MSD, the ratio to far
and the density ratio are each jackknifed directly). It does not include the small number of Li in the inner shell (n_Li is
printed: read the ratio with that in mind).

The dopant is NOT static: the shell of a Li is its distance to the dopant in every frame (dopant position per frame).
Ownership (--own, default 'start'; 'resident' = check): a window (t0, t0+tau) counts for a shell only if the Li stayed in that shell
for every frame of the window; a Li that leaves and enters another shell is owned by the new shell for the windows it spends
there. --own start = old rule (shell at t0 only, Li may have left by t0+tau). Resident is biased LOW for the inner shells
(only Li that stay are counted; at tau &amp;gt;&amp;gt; residence time the cell is empty, n_Li_resident in the csv/log says how many).
Local structure differs between regions, so a ratio far from 1 can also be geometry, not the dopant (no control region).

usage:
  python 05_msd_by_shell.py /path/to/folder
  python 05_msd_by_shell.py /path/to/folder --T 800 1200 --lags 1 5 10 --shells 3 5 7
outputs (results/&amp;lt;s&amp;gt;/): msd_shell.csv, msd_shell_ratio.png
&amp;quot;&amp;quot;&amp;quot;
import argparse
import csv
import os

import numpy as np

import llzo_io as io


def load(cfg, T, tmin):
    traj = io.prepare(cfg, io.dump_path(cfg, T), tmin)
    drift = io.framework_drift(cfg, traj)
    lm = io.li_mask(cfg, traj)
    li = traj.pos[:, lm, :] - drift[:, None, :]
    dops, cnt = [], {}
    for t in cfg[&amp;quot;dopant_types&amp;quot;]:
        for i in np.where(traj.types == t)[0]:
            el = cfg[&amp;quot;types&amp;quot;][t]
            cnt[el] = cnt.get(el, 0) + 1
            dops.append((f&amp;quot;{el}{cnt[el]}&amp;quot;, io.dopant_xyz(cfg, traj, drift, i)))      # smoothed over --dop-smooth-ps
    return li, dops, traj.L.mean(axis=0), float(np.median(np.diff(traj.time_ps)))


def shell_msd(li, sh, K, lag_frames, nblocks, own=&amp;quot;resident&amp;quot;):
    &amp;quot;&amp;quot;&amp;quot;li (F,N,3) unwrapped; sh (F,N) shell of every Li in every frame (-1 = excluded). Returns {lag: (msd (K,), se, ratio to
    far (K,), se, n_origin (K,))} with delete-one-block jackknife errors over the time origins (NaN when the lag leaves too few
    origins). own 'resident': a window counts only if the Li stayed in the shell for the whole window (io.resident_labels);
    'start': shell at t0 only. n_origin = mean number of Li per origin that were used.&amp;quot;&amp;quot;&amp;quot;
    K_ = K
    F = li.shape[0]
    out = {}
    for lag in lag_frames:
        if lag &amp;gt;= F - nblocks:
            nan = np.full(K_, np.nan)
            out[lag] = (nan, nan, nan, nan, nan)
            continue
        d2 = ((li[lag:] - li[:-lag]) ** 2).sum(axis=-1)    # (F-lag,N)
        lab = io.resident_labels(sh, lag) if own == &amp;quot;resident&amp;quot; else sh[:-lag]
        S = io.block_counts(lab, K_, nblocks, values=d2)
        C = io.block_counts(lab, K_, nblocks)
        msd, se = io.jackknife(lambda s_, c_: s_ / c_, S, C)
        rat, rse = io.jackknife(lambda s_, c_: (s_ / c_) / (s_ / c_)[-1], S, C)
        out[lag] = (msd, se, rat, rse, C.sum(0) / (F - lag))
    return out


def plot_ratio(rows, lags, names, path):
    &amp;quot;&amp;quot;&amp;quot;One panel per (dopant, tau): x = T, one line per shell (colour), y = MSD(shell)/MSD(far). One shared legend that
    also gives the mean number of Li in each shell (small n = noisy).&amp;quot;&amp;quot;&amp;quot;
    import matplotlib
    matplotlib.use(&amp;quot;Agg&amp;quot;)
    import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
    labs = sorted({r[&amp;quot;dopant&amp;quot;] for r in rows})
    K = len(names)
    cols = [plt.cm.viridis(k / max(K - 2, 1)) for k in range(K - 1)]
    fig, axs = plt.subplots(len(labs), len(lags), figsize=(4.2 * len(lags), 3.3 * len(labs) + 0.9), dpi=150,
                            squeeze=False, sharex=True, sharey=True)      # ONE y axis: panels comparable by eye
    for ri, lab in enumerate(labs):
        for ci, tau in enumerate(lags):
            a = axs[ri][ci]
            for k in range(K - 1):
                sel = [r for r in rows if r[&amp;quot;dopant&amp;quot;] == lab and r[&amp;quot;shell&amp;quot;] == names[k] and abs(r[&amp;quot;tau_ps&amp;quot;] - tau) &amp;lt; 0.06]
                if sel:
                    a.errorbar([r[&amp;quot;T&amp;quot;] for r in sel], [r[&amp;quot;ratio_to_far&amp;quot;] for r in sel], [r[&amp;quot;ratio_se&amp;quot;] for r in sel],
                               marker=&amp;quot;o&amp;quot;, ms=4, capsize=2, color=cols[k])
            a.axhline(1, color=&amp;quot;0.5&amp;quot;, lw=0.8)
            a.set_title(f&amp;quot;{lab},  tau = {tau:g} ps&amp;quot;, fontsize=10)
            if ri == len(labs) - 1:
                a.set_xlabel(&amp;quot;T (K)&amp;quot;)
            if ci == 0:
                a.set_ylabel(f&amp;quot;MSD(shell) / MSD({names[-1]})&amp;quot;)
    from matplotlib.lines import Line2D
    h = []
    for k in range(K - 1):
        n = np.mean([r[&amp;quot;n_Li&amp;quot;] for r in rows if r[&amp;quot;shell&amp;quot;] == names[k]])
        h.append(Line2D([], [], color=cols[k], marker=&amp;quot;o&amp;quot;, lw=2, label=f&amp;quot;Li starting {names[k]} from dopant (mean {n:.1f} Li)&amp;quot;))
    h.append(Line2D([], [], color=&amp;quot;0.5&amp;quot;, lw=1, label=f&amp;quot;1 = same as far Li ({names[-1]})&amp;quot;))
    fig.legend(handles=h, loc=&amp;quot;lower center&amp;quot;, ncol=2, fontsize=8, frameon=False)
    fig.suptitle(&amp;quot;Li mobility near each dopant relative to far Li  (&amp;lt; 1 = slower near the dopant; error bars = block jackknife)&amp;quot;, fontsize=10)
    fig.tight_layout(rect=(0, 0.1, 1, 0.96))
    fig.savefig(path)
    plt.close(fig)


def main():
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__, formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter)
    ap.add_argument(&amp;quot;target&amp;quot;, help=&amp;quot;folder with the dumps + element_list (or a settings .yaml)&amp;quot;)
    ap.add_argument(&amp;quot;--defaults&amp;quot;)
    ap.add_argument(&amp;quot;--T&amp;quot;, type=int, nargs=&amp;quot;*&amp;quot;)
    ap.add_argument(&amp;quot;--tmin&amp;quot;, type=float)
    ap.add_argument(&amp;quot;--outdir&amp;quot;, default=&amp;quot;results&amp;quot;)
    ap.add_argument(&amp;quot;--shells&amp;quot;, type=float, nargs=&amp;quot;*&amp;quot;, default=list(io.DEFAULT_SHELLS), help=&amp;quot;shell edges, A (same default in 04, 05, 06)&amp;quot;)
    ap.add_argument(&amp;quot;--far&amp;quot;, choices=[&amp;quot;all&amp;quot;, &amp;quot;self&amp;quot;], default=&amp;quot;all&amp;quot;,
                    help=&amp;quot;far shell = farther than the last edge from EVERY dopant (all) or from that dopant only (self)&amp;quot;)
    ap.add_argument(&amp;quot;--lags&amp;quot;, type=float, nargs=&amp;quot;*&amp;quot;, default=[1.0, 5.0, 10.0], help=&amp;quot;tau values, ps&amp;quot;)
    ap.add_argument(&amp;quot;--blocks&amp;quot;, type=int, default=io.DEFAULT_BLOCKS, help=&amp;quot;time blocks for the jackknife error bars&amp;quot;)
    ap.add_argument(&amp;quot;--own&amp;quot;, choices=[&amp;quot;resident&amp;quot;, &amp;quot;start&amp;quot;], default=&amp;quot;start&amp;quot;,
                    help=&amp;quot;resident: window counts for a shell only if the Li stays in it the whole window; start: shell at t0 (default)&amp;quot;)
    io.add_config_args(ap)
    args = ap.parse_args()

    cfg = io.load_config(args.target, args.defaults, args)
    temps = args.T or io.temperatures(cfg)
    outdir = os.path.join(args.outdir, cfg[&amp;quot;name&amp;quot;])
    os.makedirs(outdir, exist_ok=True)
    edges = np.array(args.shells, float)
    names = io.shell_names(edges)
    K = len(names)

    rows = []
    for T in temps:
        print(f&amp;quot;\n=== {cfg['name']}  T = {T} K ===&amp;quot;)
        li, dops, L, dt = load(cfg, T, args.tmin)
        if not dops:
            raise SystemExit(&amp;quot;no dopant atoms in this system: nothing to compare&amp;quot;)
        lag_frames = [max(1, int(round(x / dt))) for x in args.lags]
        F, N = li.shape[:2]
        Lf = np.broadcast_to(L, (F, 3))
        dists = {lab: io.dist_to(li, dop, Lf) for lab, dop in dops}
        dnear = np.min(list(dists.values()), axis=0)
        dists = {&amp;quot;any&amp;quot;: dnear, **dists}
        vf = io.shell_volume_fractions({lab: dop.mean(axis=0) for lab, dop in dops}, L, edges, args.far)
        frb = np.bincount(io.block_id(np.arange(F), F, args.blocks), minlength=args.blocks)[:, None].astype(float)
        for lab, d_ in dists.items():
            sh = io.shell_labels(d_, dnear, edges, args.far)
            res = shell_msd(li, sh, K, lag_frames, args.blocks, args.own)
            C = io.block_counts(sh, K, args.blocks)
            n_mean = C.sum(0) / F
            expect = N * vf[lab]
            dens, dens_se = io.jackknife(lambda c_, f_: c_ / f_ / expect, C, frb)
            print(f&amp;quot;  {lab}: Li per shell (mean) and density ratio to uniform (+/- jackknife SE)&amp;quot;)
            print(&amp;quot;    &amp;quot; + &amp;quot;   &amp;quot;.join(f&amp;quot;{names[k]}: {n_mean[k]:.2f} ({dens[k]:.2f} +/- {dens_se[k]:.2f}x)&amp;quot; for k in range(K)))
            for lag, lf in zip(args.lags, lag_frames):
                msd, se, rat, rse, nres = res[lf]
                print(f&amp;quot;    tau = {lf * dt:g} ps   MSD (A^2) +/- SE   ratio to far +/- SE   &amp;lt;Li used per origin&amp;gt; [{args.own}]&amp;quot;)
                for k in range(K):
                    print(f&amp;quot;      {names[k]:&amp;gt;8}   {msd[k]:8.3f} +/- {se[k]:.3f}   &amp;quot;
                          + (&amp;quot;     1 (reference)&amp;quot; if k == K - 1 else f&amp;quot;{rat[k]:5.2f} +/- {rse[k]:.2f}&amp;quot;) + f&amp;quot;   {nres[k]:6.2f}&amp;quot;)
                    rows.append(dict(T=T, dopant=lab, tau_ps=lf * dt, shell=names[k], n_Li=n_mean[k], n_Li_used=nres[k], own=args.own,
                                     density_ratio=dens[k],
                                     density_ratio_se=dens_se[k], msd_A2=msd[k], msd_se=se[k], ratio_to_far=rat[k],
                                     ratio_se=rse[k], blocks=args.blocks))
    with open(os.path.join(outdir, &amp;quot;msd_shell.csv&amp;quot;), &amp;quot;w&amp;quot;, newline=&amp;quot;&amp;quot;) as fh:
        w = csv.DictWriter(fh, fieldnames=list(rows[0].keys()))
        w.writeheader()
        w.writerows(rows)

    plot_ratio(rows, args.lags, names, os.path.join(outdir, &amp;quot;msd_shell_ratio.png&amp;quot;))
    print(f&amp;quot;\n[+] {outdir}/msd_shell.csv msd_shell_ratio.png&amp;quot;)
    print(&amp;quot;Read: ratio &amp;lt; 1 = Li near the dopant move less than far Li; &amp;gt; 1 = more. Check n_Li (few Li -&amp;gt; noisy) and &amp;quot;
          &amp;quot;whether the sign is the same at every tau and T.&amp;quot;)


if __name__ == &amp;quot;__main__&amp;quot;:
    main()
&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/details&gt;&lt;details class=&quot;attachment&quot;&gt;&lt;summary&gt;Attachment: 08_phonon_band_center.py&lt;/summary&gt;&lt;pre&gt;#!/usr/bin/env python3
&amp;quot;&amp;quot;&amp;quot;
08_phonon_band_center.py - Li phonon band centre from the MD dump (VACF -&amp;gt; PDOS -&amp;gt; first moment).  No sites, no hops.

Li velocities come from central differences of the unwrapped positions (framework-COM drift removed, same as 05):
    v(t) = [x(t+dt) - x(t-dt)] / 2dt
VACF of all Li (sum over atoms and xyz, unbiased, Hann-tapered over --win-ps) -&amp;gt; cosine transform -&amp;gt; g(f) (Li-projected PDOS).
    band centre  f_c = int f g(f) df / int g(f) df    over  [--fmin, fmax]   (also printed without the --fmin cut)
Central-difference attenuation sinc(2 pi f dt)^2 is divided out of g.

HARD LIMIT: dump spacing dt. Nyquist = 1/(2 dt). Li modes are ~5-15 THz, so dt must be &amp;lt;= ~10 fs. Larger dt: aliasing,
no correction possible -&amp;gt; script stops (--force overrides; the numbers are then meaningless). 100 fs dumps -&amp;gt; Nyquist 5 THz.
Fix is a new short run: NVE (or weak thermostat), e.g.  dump 2 all custom 2 dump_phonon.lammpstrj id type xu yu zu

Classical MD: no quantum occupation weighting. Hopping adds a low-f diffusive part to g (pulls f_c down): compare systems
and temperatures with the same protocol; the --fmin cut (default 1 THz) removes most of it and is reported separately.
Checks printed: f_c at 2x VACF window (should not move) and block standard error (--blocks contiguous segments).

usage:
  python 08_phonon_band_center.py /path/to/folder
  python 08_phonon_band_center.py /path/to/folder --T 800 1200 --win-ps 3 --fmin 1
  python 08_phonon_band_center.py /path/to/run --dump-pattern 'MD-{T}/dump.{T}.lammpstrj' --elements Li La Zr Al O --fold 2 2 2
(system-layout flags --dump-dir --prefix --dump-pattern --elements --element-list --dopants --fold --name as in 01-07)
outputs (&amp;lt;outdir&amp;gt;/&amp;lt;system&amp;gt;/): phonon_band_center.csv, pdos_Li.png
&amp;quot;&amp;quot;&amp;quot;
import argparse
import csv
import os
import sys

import numpy as np

import llzo_io as io

THZ_TO_MEV = 4.135667696


def load(cfg, T, tmin):
    traj = io.prepare(cfg, io.dump_path(cfg, T), tmin)
    drift = io.framework_drift(cfg, traj)
    lm = io.li_mask(cfg, traj)
    li = traj.pos[:, lm, :] - drift[:, None, :]
    return li, float(np.median(np.diff(traj.time_ps)))


def pdos(v, dt_ps, win):
    &amp;quot;&amp;quot;&amp;quot;v (F,N,3). Returns f (THz), g (not normalised). VACF window = win frames.&amp;quot;&amp;quot;&amp;quot;
    n = v.shape[0]
    f = np.fft.rfft(v, n=2 * n, axis=0)
    acf = np.fft.irfft(f * np.conj(f), axis=0)[:win].sum(axis=(1, 2))
    acf = acf / (n - np.arange(win))
    acf = acf / acf[0] * np.hanning(2 * win)[win:]
    sym = np.concatenate([acf, acf[-2:0:-1]])
    freq = np.fft.rfftfreq(len(sym), dt_ps)
    g = np.clip(np.fft.rfft(sym).real, 0, None)
    x = 2 * np.pi * freq * dt_ps                       # central difference over 2 dt: amplitude sin(x)/x
    ok = np.sinc(x / np.pi) &amp;gt; 0.3
    g = np.where(ok, g / np.where(ok, np.sinc(x / np.pi) ** 2, 1.0), 0.0)
    return freq, g


def centre(freq, g, fmin, fmax):
    m = (freq &amp;gt;= fmin) &amp;amp; (freq &amp;lt;= fmax)
    return float((freq[m] * g[m]).sum() / g[m].sum())


def velocities(li, dt_ps):
    return (li[2:] - li[:-2]) / (2 * dt_ps)


def analyse(li, dt_ps, win_ps, fmin, fmax, nblocks):
    v = velocities(li, dt_ps)
    win = max(8, int(round(win_ps / dt_ps)))
    if 4 * win &amp;gt; v.shape[0]:
        raise SystemExit(f&amp;quot;trajectory ({v.shape[0]} frames) too short for window {win} frames; lower --win-ps&amp;quot;)
    f, g = pdos(v, dt_ps, win)
    f2, g2 = pdos(v, dt_ps, min(2 * win, v.shape[0] // 2))
    fc, fc_nocut = centre(f, g, fmin, fmax), centre(f, g, 0.0, fmax)
    fc2 = centre(f2, g2, fmin, fmax)
    seg = v.shape[0] // nblocks
    if seg &amp;gt;= 4 * win:
        b = [centre(*pdos(v[k * seg:(k + 1) * seg], dt_ps, win), fmin, fmax) for k in range(nblocks)]
        se = float(np.std(b, ddof=1) / np.sqrt(nblocks))
    else:
        se = float(&amp;quot;nan&amp;quot;)
    return f, g, fc, fc_nocut, fc2, se, win


def main():
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__, formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter)
    ap.add_argument(&amp;quot;target&amp;quot;, help=&amp;quot;folder with the dumps + element_list (or a settings .yaml)&amp;quot;)
    ap.add_argument(&amp;quot;--defaults&amp;quot;)
    ap.add_argument(&amp;quot;--T&amp;quot;, type=int, nargs=&amp;quot;*&amp;quot;)
    ap.add_argument(&amp;quot;--tmin&amp;quot;, type=float)
    ap.add_argument(&amp;quot;--outdir&amp;quot;, default=&amp;quot;phonon08&amp;quot;)
    ap.add_argument(&amp;quot;--win-ps&amp;quot;, type=float, default=3.0, help=&amp;quot;VACF window, ps (frequency resolution ~ 1/win)&amp;quot;)
    ap.add_argument(&amp;quot;--fmin&amp;quot;, type=float, default=1.0, help=&amp;quot;THz, lower cut of the band-centre integral (hopping tail)&amp;quot;)
    ap.add_argument(&amp;quot;--fmax&amp;quot;, type=float, default=30.0, help=&amp;quot;THz, upper limit (also capped at 0.8 x Nyquist)&amp;quot;)
    ap.add_argument(&amp;quot;--blocks&amp;quot;, type=int, default=4, help=&amp;quot;contiguous segments for the standard error&amp;quot;)
    ap.add_argument(&amp;quot;--max-dt-fs&amp;quot;, type=float, default=10.0, help=&amp;quot;largest dump spacing accepted&amp;quot;)
    ap.add_argument(&amp;quot;--force&amp;quot;, action=&amp;quot;store_true&amp;quot;, help=&amp;quot;run even if dt is too large (aliased, meaningless)&amp;quot;)
    io.add_config_args(ap)
    args = ap.parse_args()

    cfg = io.load_config(args.target, args.defaults, args)
    temps = args.T or io.temperatures(cfg)
    outdir = os.path.join(args.outdir, cfg[&amp;quot;name&amp;quot;])
    os.makedirs(outdir, exist_ok=True)

    rows, spectra = [], {}
    for T in temps:
        print(f&amp;quot;\n=== {cfg['name']}  T = {T} K ===&amp;quot;)
        li, dt_ps = load(cfg, T, args.tmin)
        dt_fs = dt_ps * 1e3
        nyq = 0.5 / dt_ps
        print(f&amp;quot;  dump spacing {dt_fs:g} fs -&amp;gt; Nyquist {nyq:.1f} THz;  {li.shape[1]} Li, {li.shape[0]} frames&amp;quot;)
        if dt_fs &amp;gt; args.max_dt_fs and not args.force:
            print(f&amp;quot;  STOP: dt &amp;gt; {args.max_dt_fs:g} fs. Li modes (~5-15 THz) are above Nyquist: PDOS aliased, band centre meaningless.\n&amp;quot;
                  f&amp;quot;  Rerun a short NVE with dt &amp;lt;= 5 fs dumps, e.g.  dump 2 all custom 2 dump_phonon.lammpstrj id type xu yu zu\n&amp;quot;
                  f&amp;quot;  (or --force to compute anyway).&amp;quot;)
            sys.exit(2)
        fmax = min(args.fmax, 0.8 * nyq)
        f, g, fc, fc_nc, fc2, se, win = analyse(li, dt_ps, args.win_ps, args.fmin, fmax, args.blocks)
        print(f&amp;quot;  VACF window {win} frames = {win * dt_ps:g} ps;  band [{args.fmin:g}, {fmax:g}] THz&amp;quot;)
        print(f&amp;quot;  f_c = {fc:.3f} +/- {se:.3f} THz ({fc * THZ_TO_MEV:.2f} meV)   without fmin cut: {fc_nc:.3f} THz   &amp;quot;
              f&amp;quot;at 2x window: {fc2:.3f} THz&amp;quot;)
        rows.append(dict(T=T, dt_fs=dt_fs, n_Li=li.shape[1], win_ps=win * dt_ps, fmin_THz=args.fmin, fmax_THz=fmax,
                         fc_THz=fc, fc_se_THz=se, fc_meV=fc * THZ_TO_MEV, fc_nocut_THz=fc_nc, fc_2xwin_THz=fc2))
        m = f &amp;lt;= fmax
        spectra[T] = (f[m], g[m] / (0.5 * (g[m][1:] + g[m][:-1]) * np.diff(f[m])).sum())

    with open(os.path.join(outdir, &amp;quot;phonon_band_center.csv&amp;quot;), &amp;quot;w&amp;quot;, newline=&amp;quot;&amp;quot;) as fh:
        w = csv.DictWriter(fh, fieldnames=list(rows[0].keys()))
        w.writeheader()
        w.writerows(rows)

    import matplotlib
    matplotlib.use(&amp;quot;Agg&amp;quot;)
    import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
    fig, axs = plt.subplots(1, 2, figsize=(10, 3.8), dpi=150)
    cm = plt.cm.plasma(np.linspace(0.05, 0.85, len(spectra)))
    for c, (T, (f, g)) in zip(cm, spectra.items()):
        axs[0].plot(f, g, color=c, label=f&amp;quot;{T} K&amp;quot;)
    axs[0].set_xlabel(&amp;quot;f (THz)&amp;quot;)
    axs[0].set_ylabel(&amp;quot;Li PDOS (area = 1)&amp;quot;)
    axs[0].legend(fontsize=8)
    axs[1].errorbar([r[&amp;quot;T&amp;quot;] for r in rows], [r[&amp;quot;fc_THz&amp;quot;] for r in rows], [r[&amp;quot;fc_se_THz&amp;quot;] for r in rows], marker=&amp;quot;o&amp;quot;, capsize=2)
    axs[1].set_xlabel(&amp;quot;T (K)&amp;quot;)
    axs[1].set_ylabel(&amp;quot;Li band centre (THz)&amp;quot;)
    fig.suptitle(f&amp;quot;{cfg['name']}: Li phonon band centre (VACF, classical MD)&amp;quot;, fontsize=10)
    fig.tight_layout()
    fig.savefig(os.path.join(outdir, &amp;quot;pdos_Li.png&amp;quot;))
    plt.close(fig)
    print(f&amp;quot;\n[+] {outdir}/phonon_band_center.csv pdos_Li.png&amp;quot;)
    print(&amp;quot;Read: lower f_c = softer Li well. Trust only if 'at 2x window' ~ f_c and SE is small. Same protocol for every system.&amp;quot;)


if __name__ == &amp;quot;__main__&amp;quot;:
    main()
&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/details&gt;&lt;details class=&quot;attachment&quot;&gt;&lt;summary&gt;Attachment: 09_shell_haven.py&lt;/summary&gt;&lt;pre&gt;#!/usr/bin/env python3
&amp;quot;&amp;quot;&amp;quot;
09_shell_haven.py - Haven ratio per distance shell around the dopant (0-3, 3-5, 5-7, &amp;gt;7 A by default). Dumps only, NO sites.

Same shells, 'any'/per-dopant references, far definition and block-jackknife errors as 05 (llzo_io.py).
The dopant is NOT static: the shell of a Li is its distance to the dopant position in every frame. Ownership (--own, default
'start', as in 05): the shell at t0 owns the whole window (Li may have left by t0+tau; use tau shorter than the shell residence time).
--own resident = check: a window counts only if the Li stayed in that shell for every frame of it; a Li that leaves and enters another
shell is owned by the new shell for the windows it spends there. Resident conditions on staying (survivor bias, inner shells biased low; &amp;lt;Li&amp;gt; below is then the mean number of Li USED per
origin, so the --min-n flag applies to the Li that really enter the sums).
For one shell S (the Li owned by S for the window) and one lag tau:
    tracer      MSD_tr  = &amp;lt; sum_{i in S} |dr_i|^2 &amp;gt;_t0                          dr_i = r_i(t0+tau) - r_i(t0)
    collective  MSD_col = &amp;lt; | sum_{i in S} dr_i |^2 &amp;gt;_t0                        (the SAME Li, summed vector first)
    H_R(S, tau) = MSD_tr / MSD_col  =  D*_S / D_sigma,S                          (the N_S in D_sigma = MSD_col / (6 N_S t) cancels)
H_R = 1: the Li of the shell move independently; &amp;lt; 1: their displacements are positively correlated (concerted / knock-on).
Also computed for ALL Li together with the same tau (grey line in the figure) - the reference to compare the shells with.
Error bars: delete-one-block jackknife over --blocks time blocks (numerator and denominator are additive sums per block).

READ THIS BEFORE TRUSTING A NUMBER
 * It is an INTRA-shell Haven ratio: correlations between a Li inside the shell and a Li in another shell are not in it. It says
   &amp;quot;do the Li that start in this shell move together&amp;quot;, not &amp;quot;what is the Haven ratio of the material there&amp;quot;. (A true local Haven
   ratio does not exist: sigma is a property of the whole sample.)
 * (shell = START shell in the old rule, --own start; with --own resident the Li stay, see above.)
 * A shell with n Li on average: for n ~ 1 the sum is one vector, so MSD_col ~ MSD_tr and H_R -&amp;gt; 1 by construction. Cells with a
   mean of fewer than --min-n Li are drawn as OPEN symbols; do not read a number from them. Ga 0-3 A holds ~0-3 Li.
 * H_R(tau) is a ratio at ONE lag, not a slope fit as in 03. At short tau (a few vibrational periods) both MSDs are the same
   vibration, so H_R -&amp;gt; 1; the value converges (if it does) only for tau &amp;gt;&amp;gt; the hop time. Compare shells at the same tau and look at
   the largest tau. At large tau the Li have left the shell they started in (the shell is the START shell), mixing the shells.
 * D_sigma is one collective variable and is much noisier than D*: with 5 blocks the SE itself is only a rough scale.
 * Li are removed from a shell only by moving; there is no exclusion of Li near the box edge beyond the minimum image.

usage:
  python 09_shell_haven.py /path/to/folder [--T 800 1200] [--lags 2 5 10 20] [--shells 3 5 7] [--far all|self]
  (same system flags as the other scripts: --elements --dopants --dump-pattern ...)
outputs (&amp;lt;outdir&amp;gt;/&amp;lt;system&amp;gt;/): haven_shell.csv, haven_shell.png (+ .svg copies)
&amp;quot;&amp;quot;&amp;quot;
import argparse
import csv
import os

import numpy as np

import llzo_io as io


def load(cfg, T, tmin):
    traj = io.prepare(cfg, io.dump_path(cfg, T), tmin)
    drift = io.framework_drift(cfg, traj)
    lm = io.li_mask(cfg, traj)
    li = traj.pos[:, lm, :] - drift[:, None, :]
    dops, cnt = [], {}
    for t in cfg[&amp;quot;dopant_types&amp;quot;]:
        for i in np.where(traj.types == t)[0]:
            el = cfg[&amp;quot;types&amp;quot;][t]
            cnt[el] = cnt.get(el, 0) + 1
            dops.append((f&amp;quot;{el}{cnt[el]}&amp;quot;, io.dopant_xyz(cfg, traj, drift, i)))      # smoothed over --dop-smooth-ps
    return li, dops, traj.L.mean(axis=0), float(np.median(np.diff(traj.time_ps)))


def shell_sums(d, sh, K, nblocks):
